    match_type = "direct_match", "prob_match", or "unmatched".
    prob_match_similarity contains the score for prob_match rows.

CSV FILES (conditional):

  {prefix}_prob_assignments_{label}.csv
    Written only when at least one probabilistic pair was accepted.
    One row per accepted pair with: adm_uid, adm_facility, dis_uid,
    dis_facility, overall_similarity, and one column per scoring variable.

  {prefix}_prob_candidates_{label}.csv
    Written only when at least one candidate was found (above min_similarity,
    before one-to-one assignment).
    One row per candidate pair — useful for reviewing near-misses and
    cases where a close second candidate existed.

  {prefix}_duplicates_log.csv
    Written only when duplicates were found in admissions or discharges.
    Contains all duplicate records with action (KEPT/REMOVED) and
    possible_readmission flag.

TEXT REPORTS (always written):

  {prefix}_matching_statistics_{label}.txt
    Comprehensive overview of the entire Pipeline 1 run.  See below.

  {prefix}_prob_matching_report_{label}.txt
    Written only when probabilistic matching ran (even if no pairs accepted).
    Detailed report on the probabilistic step.  See below.


================================================================================
MATCHING STATISTICS REPORT — SECTIONS
================================================================================

1. METADATA
   Run date/time, script name, R version, config settings used
   (country, source, cleaning, dates, facility filter, prob matching settings).

2. INPUT FILES
   Paths to admissions and discharges files.
   Raw row counts before any processing.
   Column counts.

3. ADMISSION DATE FILTER
   Mode (simple / advanced), dates applied, facility filter, auto-mode flag.
   Rows before and after filtering; rows excluded.
   Variable filter (if applied): column, values, rows excluded.

4. DEDUPLICATION
   Number of duplicate keys found in admissions and discharges.
   Number of rows removed from each.

5. DIRECT MATCHING SUMMARY
   Total admissions and discharges after deduplication.
   Matched pairs count with percentage of admissions and discharges matched.
   Unmatched admissions and discharges with percentages.

6. OUTCOME DISTRIBUTION  (neotreeoutcome)
   Frequency table of neotreeoutcome values among matched pairs, sorted
   by frequency, with counts and percentages.
   Per-facility breakdown of matched pairs.

7. UNMATCHED BREAKDOWN
   Per-facility counts of unmatched admissions and unmatched discharges.

8. MASTER DATASETS SUMMARY
   Row counts and match_type breakdown for master_joined and master_joined_extended.
   prob_match_similarity statistics (min, mean, max) for prob_match rows.
   Row count and direct_match / prob_match split for joined_admissions_discharges_extended.

9. OUTPUT FILES
   Full paths of every file written, with file sizes.


================================================================================
PROBABILISTIC MATCHING REPORT — SECTIONS
================================================================================

1. METADATA
   Run date/time, script name.

2. CONFIGURATION
   All prob_match_* settings from config.R used in this run.

3. VARIABLE SELECTION
   For each candidate variable: whether it was included or excluded and why.
   For included variables: completeness % in admissions and discharges files,
   tolerance used.

4. CANDIDATE SEARCH
   Number of unmatched admissions and discharges searched.
   Number of pairs scored.
   Number of candidates above the minimum similarity threshold.
   Whether cross-facility search was used.

5. ONE-TO-ONE ASSIGNMENT
   Number of pairs accepted.
   Similarity score distribution (min, mean, max, quartiles).

6. PER-VARIABLE SCORE BREAKDOWN
   For each accepted assignment: mean score per variable across all accepted
   pairs.  Shows which variables were the most discriminating.

7. MASTER_JOINED_EXTENDED SUMMARY
   Row counts by match_type in the extended dataset.
   Coverage improvement vs master_joined (additional % of admissions
   with discharge data after probabilistic matching).

8. TOP-20 ASSIGNMENTS
   Table of the 20 accepted pairs with highest overall similarity,
   showing uid pairs, facilities, similarity, and key variable scores.

9. OUTPUT FILES
   Paths of all prob-related files written.


================================================================================
INTERNAL HELPERS
================================================================================

  .build_stats_report(cfg, ps, output_paths)
    Generates the matching statistics report as a character vector of lines.

  .build_prob_report(cfg, ps, output_paths)
    Generates the probabilistic matching report.

  .fac_breakdown(df, label)
    Formats a per-facility count table as report lines.

All helpers are internal (prefixed with ".") and not called directly.


================================================================================
DEPENDENCIES
================================================================================

  - Base R only (write.csv, writeLines, dir.create, file.path, format,
    Sys.time, R.version.string, table, range)
  - No other modules


================================================================================


================================================================================
  6.9  subsample_maker.R -- filtering and column selection
================================================================================
  Source file: sample_maker_R/modules/README_subsample_maker.txt
================================================================================

================================================================================
  Neotree Sample Maker — modules/subsample_maker.R
  README: Subsample filtering, column selection, and report writing
================================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
subsample_maker.R provides all the logic for Pipeline 2 (run_subsample_maker.R).
It reads master_joined and master_joined_extended, applies an admission date
filter and optional facility/column selection, and writes the resulting
subsamples and a detailed text report.

This module is NOT part of Pipeline 1 (run_sample_maker.R).  It is sourced
exclusively by run_subsample_maker.R.


================================================================================
EXPORTED FUNCTIONS
================================================================================

1.  run_subsample_maker(master_joined, master_joined_extended, cfg)
    Applies all filters and column selection to both master datasets.
    Returns a sub_result list.

2.  write_subsample_outputs(sub_result, cfg, prefix, out_dir, na_coded = NULL)
    Writes four subsample CSVs and the text report.
    When na_coded is a named list (joined, extended, joined_matched,
    extended_matched), also writes four paired *_na_coded.csv files.
    Returns a named list of all output file paths.


================================================================================
FUNCTION DETAILS
================================================================================

----------------------------------------------------------------------
run_subsample_maker(master_joined, master_joined_extended, cfg)
----------------------------------------------------------------------

The function processes both master datasets in parallel using the same
configuration settings.  The cfg argument is the SUBSAMPLE_CONFIG list
from run_subsample_maker.R.

STEP A — ENSURE PARSED DATE
  Re-parses admission dates if adm_date_parsed is absent or not POSIXct.
  This handles the common case where master datasets are read from CSV
  (which drops POSIXct metadata, storing dates as character strings).
  Falls back to parsing datetimeadmission with the same two-format strategy
  used in filter_data.R (full datetime, then date-only).
  If datetimeadmission itself is missing, date filtering is skipped.

STEP B — RESOLVE FILTER INFO
  Calls .build_sub_filter_info() to determine the filter label and description.
  The label encodes the date window and facility selection:
    "to_20260228", "20240101_to_20260228", "ALL", "SMCH", etc.
  When column selection is active (sub_variables not NULL), "_Nvars" is
  appended to the label (e.g. "_12vars").

STEP C — APPLY FILTER (for each dataset)
  .apply_sub_filter() subsets rows based on cfg$sub_use_advanced_mode:

  SIMPLE MODE:
    Applies sub_start_date, sub_end_date, and sub_facility_filter.
    Same logic as apply_admission_filter() in filter_data.R.
    Rows where adm_date_parsed is NA are excluded when date bounds are set.

  ADVANCED MODE:
    Per-facility date ranges from sub_facility_date_ranges.
    Same logic as advanced mode in filter_data.R.

  Returns a metadata list per dataset:
    n_total, n_kept, n_excluded, n_missing_dates, date_min, date_max,
    filter_label, type_tab (match_type frequency table).

STEP D — APPLY EXCLUSION FILTERS (for each dataset)
  .apply_exclusion_filters() removes rows where a variable meets a condition.
  Applied to each dataset independently after the date/facility filter.

  cfg$sub_exclusion_filters is a list of entries:
    list(variable = "colname", operator = "<", value = 24)
  Supported operators: "<", "<=", ">", ">=", "==", "!=", "in", "not_in"

  Rows where the filter variable is NA are KEPT (conservative default).
  Numeric coercion is applied when the comparison value is numeric.
  Multiple filters are applied in sequence; a row is excluded if it matches
  any single filter.
  When any filters are set, "_excl" is appended to the output label.

  Returns: list(df, excl_info, n_total_excluded)
    excl_info  -- per-filter result: variable, operator, value, n_excluded,
                  skipped (TRUE if column not found)

STEP E — SELECT COLUMNS (for each dataset)
  .select_columns() restricts the data frame to the requested column set.

  MANDATORY COLUMNS (always retained regardless of sub_variables):
    uid, facility, uniquekey, datetimeadmission, match_key, adm_date_parsed,
    match_type, prob_match_similarity

  Requested columns (sub_variables) are matched by exact name against the
  data frame columns.  Names not found are recorded in a "not_found" list
  and reported in the subsample report.

  Returns the reduced data frame plus col_info:
    requested, found, not_found, mandatory, final_cols, n_vars

STEP G — DERIVE MATCHED-ONLY DATASETS (always)
  Two matched-only datasets are always derived, mirroring the Pipeline 1
  naming convention:

  subsample_joined_matched_only
    Derived from subsample_joined by keeping only rows where
    match_type == "direct_match".  Mirrors joined_admissions_discharges.

  subsample_joined_extended_matched_only
    Derived from subsample_joined_extended by removing rows where
    match_type == "unmatched".  Contains direct_match and prob_match rows.
    Mirrors joined_admissions_discharges_extended.

STEP H — RETURN
  Returns a sub_result list:
    $subsample_joined                        All admissions (master_joined filtered)
    $subsample_joined_extended               All admissions + prob matches (master_joined_extended filtered)
    $subsample_joined_matched_only           Direct matches only
    $subsample_joined_extended_matched_only  Direct + prob matches only
    $meta_joined                             Date/facility filter results for master_joined
    $meta_extended                           Date/facility filter results for master_joined_extended
    $excl_joined                             Exclusion filter results for master_joined
    $excl_extended                           Exclusion filter results for master_joined_extended
    $sub_filter_desc                         Filter description lines (for report)
    $sub_label                               Output file label string
    $col_info                                Column selection metadata

----------------------------------------------------------------------
write_subsample_outputs(sub_result, cfg, prefix, out_dir, na_coded = NULL)
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Writes five files to out_dir:

  {prefix}_subsample_master_{label}.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_master_extended_{label}.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_master_matched_only_{label}.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_master_extended_matched_only_{label}.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_report_{label}.txt

When na_coded is a named list (joined, extended, joined_matched,
extended_matched) — supplied when cfg$output_na_coded = TRUE — four
additional files are written alongside the standard CSVs:

  {prefix}_subsample_master_{label}_na_coded.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_master_extended_{label}_na_coded.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_master_matched_only_{label}_na_coded.csv
  {prefix}_subsample_master_extended_matched_only_{label}_na_coded.csv

The label is taken from sub_result$sub_label.
The out_dir is created automatically if needed.
write.csv() is used with row.names = FALSE.

Returns a named list of all output file paths (five or nine entries):
  $sub_joined, $sub_extended, $sub_joined_matched, $sub_extended_matched,
  $report  [always]
  $sub_joined_nc, $sub_extended_nc, $sub_joined_matched_nc,
  $sub_extended_matched_nc  [when na_coded is supplied]


================================================================================
SUBSAMPLE REPORT — SECTIONS
================================================================================

1. METADATA
   Run date/time, script, input file paths, row counts.

2. INPUT FILES
   master_joined and master_joined_extended dimensions.

3. FILTER APPLIED
   Mode (simple / advanced), date bounds, facility filter.
   Full description of what was applied.

4. FILTER RESULTS
   For each dataset (joined and extended):
     Rows before / kept / excluded.
     Rows with missing dates (if any).
     Date range of kept rows.
     Distribution of match_type among kept rows.

4b. EXCLUSION FILTER RESULTS
   For each filter entry: variable, operator, value, rows removed.
   Total rows removed by exclusion filters, and rows remaining.
   Section omitted from report when no exclusion filters are configured.

5. COLUMN SELECTION
   Whether column selection was active.
   Variables requested, found, not found.
   Mandatory columns always retained.
   Final column count.

6. FACILITY BREAKDOWN
   Per-facility row counts for subsample_joined and subsample_joined_extended.

6b. MATCHED-ONLY OUTPUTS
    Stats for both matched-only files, always produced:
    - subsample_joined_matched_only: row count (direct matches only).
    - subsample_joined_extended_matched_only: row count, direct/prob split,
      number of unmatched rows removed from subsample_joined_extended.

7. OUTCOME DISTRIBUTIONS
   neotreeoutcome distribution (if column present) for each output dataset,
   broken down by match_type.

8. OUTPUT FILES
   Full paths and sizes of all CSVs (four standard + up to four na_coded)
   and this report.


================================================================================
MANDATORY COLUMNS
================================================================================

The following columns are ALWAYS included in subsample outputs, even if not
listed in sub_variables, because they are required for data linkage, audit,
and downstream analysis:

  uid                    Patient identifier
  facility               Facility name
  uniquekey              Neotree record identifier (if present)
  datetimeadmission      Original admission datetime string
  match_key              uid + "_" + facility composite key
  adm_date_parsed        Parsed admission date (POSIXct)
  match_type             "direct_match", "prob_match", or "unmatched"
  prob_match_similarity  Similarity score for prob_match rows


================================================================================
RELATIONSHIP TO PIPELINE 1
================================================================================

run_sample_maker.R (Pipeline 1)
    Produces: master_joined, master_joined_extended

run_subsample_maker.R / subsample_maker.R (Pipeline 2)
    Reads:    master_joined, master_joined_extended
    Produces: subsample_joined, subsample_joined_extended, subsample_report

The separation means:
  - Pipeline 1 is run infrequently (when new data arrives)
  - Pipeline 2 can be run many times with different date windows or column
    sets, without repeating the join and probabilistic matching steps


================================================================================
DEPENDENCIES
================================================================================

  - Base R only (read.csv, write.csv, as.POSIXct, table, range, Sys.time)
  - No other modules
  - Not sourced by run_sample_maker.R — standalone use only


================================================================================


================================================================================
  6.10  anonymizer.R -- de-identification module
================================================================================
  Source file: sample_maker_R/modules/README_anonymizer.txt
================================================================================

================================================================================
  Neotree Sample Maker — modules/anonymizer.R
  README: De-identification of pipeline output files
================================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
anonymizer.R provides the logic for run_anonymizer.R.  It removes direct
identifiers, strips pipeline-internal columns, converts exact datetimes to
year-month strings, and optionally adds a sequential anonymous row ID.

This module is sourced exclusively by run_anonymizer.R.  It is not part of
Pipeline 1 or Pipeline 2.


================================================================================
EXPORTED FUNCTIONS
================================================================================

1.  anonymize_dataset(df, cfg)
    Applies all anonymization rules to a single data frame.
    Returns a named list describing what was removed and added.

2.  write_anonymizer_outputs(results_list, cfg, out_dir)
    Writes CSV outputs and a combined text report.
    Called internally by run_anonymizer.R — not called directly by the user.


================================================================================
CONFIGURABLE CONSTANTS (edit here to change built-in behaviour)
================================================================================

These three constants are defined at the top of anonymizer.R and control
what the module does to every file it processes, regardless of what the user
specifies in run_anonymizer.R.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
ANON_REMOVE_ALWAYS
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Direct identifiers that are unconditionally removed from every output:
  uid           Patient identifier
  uniquekey     Neotree record key
  match_key     uid + facility composite key (reconstructable identifier)

To add a column to this list, append its name here.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
ANON_REMOVE_PIPELINE_COLS
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Pipeline-internal columns that are always removed:
  adm_date_parsed    POSIXct version of datetimeadmission, redundant after
                     the datetime is converted to adm_yearmonth.

----------------------------------------------------------------------
ANON_DATETIME_CONVERT
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Named list mapping original datetime column → anonymized year-month column:
  datetimeadmission  →  adm_yearmonth
  datetimedischarge  →  dis_yearmonth

The new year-month column is inserted immediately after the original column
in the output, and the original column is removed.  Format: "YYYY-MM".

To add a datetime column from your data, add an entry here:
  myanydatetime = "myanydatetime_yearmonth"


================================================================================
FUNCTION DETAILS
================================================================================

----------------------------------------------------------------------
anonymize_dataset(df, cfg)
----------------------------------------------------------------------

Arguments:
  df    A data frame (any pipeline output CSV, loaded with read.csv).
  cfg   The ANONYMIZER_CONFIG list from run_anonymizer.R.
        Used fields: cfg$additional_remove, cfg$add_anon_id.

Processing steps (in order):

  1. Remove ANON_REMOVE_ALWAYS columns (if present).
  2. Remove ANON_REMOVE_PIPELINE_COLS columns (if present).
  3. Remove cfg$additional_remove columns (if present; missing names warned).
  4. Convert each column in ANON_DATETIME_CONVERT:
       - Parse with full datetime format; fall back to date-only.
       - Create a new YYYY-MM column in-place (same position).
       - Remove the original datetime column.
  5. If cfg$add_anon_id = TRUE: prepend anon_id = 1, 2, 3, … as first column.

Returns a named list:
  $df             Anonymized data frame
  $removed        Character vector of all column names removed (including
                  those replaced by year-month columns)
  $converted      Character vector of "original → new" conversion descriptions
  $added          Character vector of columns added (e.g. "anon_id")
  $n_rows         Number of rows (unchanged by anonymization)
  $n_cols_before  Number of columns before anonymization
  $n_cols_after   Number of columns after anonymization

----------------------------------------------------------------------
write_anonymizer_outputs(results_list, cfg, out_dir)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
Internal helper called by run_anonymizer.R.  Writes the anonymization report
using .build_anon_report().  Not called directly.


================================================================================
ANONYMIZATION REPORT SECTIONS
================================================================================

anonymization_report.txt contains four sections:

1. METADATA
   Run date/time, script name, add_anon_id setting.

2. VARIABLES REMOVED
   Lists all built-in removals (direct identifiers, pipeline cols, datetime
   conversions) and any user-specified additional_remove entries.
   Also lists any additional_remove names that were not found in the data.

3. FILES PROCESSED
   For each input file: row count, column counts before/after, columns removed,
   whether anon_id was added.

4. OUTPUT FILES
   Paths and file sizes of all _anon.csv files and the report itself.


================================================================================
DEPENDENCIES
================================================================================

  - Base R only (no external packages)
  - Not sourced by run_sample_maker.R or run_subsample_maker.R

================================================================================

================================================================================
  7.1  Neotree variable issues catalogue
================================================================================
  Source file: Neotree_variable_issues_catalogue.md

# Neotree data files — catalogue of variable-level issues

**Compiled:** 16 July 2026
**Scope:** issues affecting individual variables in the raw, cleaned and master Neotree data files (ZIM/SMCH and MWI/KCH), as documented across the `Neotree-data-pipeline` folder, the pipeline documentation set, the categorical-harmonisation audit, the post-implementation fix records, and the archived investigation chats.
**Purpose:** a single reference so that any analysis using cleaned or master Neotree data handles known variable problems consistently.

**Maintenance protocol — read before editing.** This is the authoritative catalogue of known variable-level issues; it must be updated whenever (a) a new variable issue is found, or (b) a mechanism described here changes — a fix is applied, a status moves (e.g. OPEN -> FIXED), or a range/threshold/mapping changes. When updating, add a dated row to the Changelog below and update **Last updated**.

**Last updated:** 17 July 2026

### Changelog

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| 2026-07-17 | **Dictionary documentation enrichment from the 2024 public dictionary.** New pass `00_build_dictionary/00e_enrich_from_public_dictionary.r` annotates each dictionary's Variables sheet with eight documentation columns (`public_meaning`, `public_data_type`, `public_dependency`, `public_range`, `old_variable_name`, `timeline_of_change`, `public_reference`, `public_dict_matched`) sourced from `og_dictionaries/Public_data_dictionary_2024.xlsx`. **Documentation only** — ValueMaps, canonical codes, ranges and cleaning behaviour are untouched (verified). Runs after `00c` in the build order; writes to `dictionary_*_enriched.xlsx`. Coverage 1,048/2,526 variables (the 2024 dict predates some fields and NeoLab uses different names). Captures variable renames (e.g. `birthweight` ← old `BW`), addressing the version-drift documentation gap. |
| 2026-07-17 | **Categorical clinical-decision layer encoded** (Sections A1, C1–C4). the clinical lead's replies + the 2024 public data dictionary were encoded into `VALUEMAP_PATCHES` in `00_build_dictionary.r` for `inorout`, `modedelivery('1')`, `admittedfrom (ER=External Referral)`, `vomiting`, `murmur`, `punewborn`, `matsymptoms`, `matcomorbidities`, `receivedantenatalcare`, `cadredis`, `admreason`, `admreasonadd`, `diagdis1`. Dictionaries rebuilt (18/18) and verified against the 25 Jun extract (ZIM adm/dis/baseline, MWI adm/dis): every mapping fires, brace multi-selects resolve (e.g. `{G,LBW,Prem,RDS}→{GSchis,LBW,Prem,DIB}`), and decision-free values remain untouched. Un-mappable / ambiguous values (admreason `Safe`/`Safekeeping`/`Hyperth`/`HIVLR`; admreasonadd `Mec`/`Cong`; diagdis1 `HBW`/`JAUN`) are listed as open clinical follow-ups. |
| 2026-07-16 | Initial catalogue compiled (Sections A–H, incl. derived combined variables in E). |

---

## How to read this document

Each issue notes the **variable(s)** affected, the **datasets/sites**, the **root cause**, the **current status** (whether the pipeline already fixes it or the analyst must handle it), and the **action** for an analyst.

Status legend:

- **FIXED** — the cleaning pipeline now handles this; cleaned/master outputs regenerated after the fix are correct.
- **PARTIAL** — a fix exists but has caveats, or has only been applied to some datasets/versions.
- **OPEN — analyst must handle** — no automatic fix; the analysis script must clean the variable.
- **OPEN — awaiting clinical decision** — cannot be resolved without a mapping decision from the clinical team.

A recurring root cause runs through most of these issues: **multiple versions of the Neotree app script have been deployed over the years at each site, and the PostgreSQL export concatenates data from all versions.** Older versions stored answers differently (text labels vs numeric codes, `true/false` vs `Yes/No`, inline descriptive labels, now-retired option codes), so a single column can carry several encodings of the same clinical concept.

---

## A. Systemic / cross-cutting issues

### A1. Categorical values not reduced to canonical codes (292 variables)

**Variables:** 292 categorical variables across all cleaned datasets, both sites.
**Status:** PARTIAL — decision-free harmonisation layer implemented; clinical-decision layer outstanding.

This is the single largest data-quality issue. The cleaning pipeline was passing raw option **labels** and legacy values straight through instead of reducing every value to the dictionary's canonical **code**. As a result the same clinical answer appears in several forms in the cleaned data. It surfaced during the LBW/SVN analysis, where scripts had to be patched to absorb the variation (antenatal steroids, maternal HIV test, place of birth), which prompted a systematic audit (`Categorical_harmonisation_audit_20260612.xlsx`).

Illustrative examples from the 10 June 2026 extract:

| Variable | Values seen in cleaned data | Should be |
|---|---|---|
| `inorout` (place of birth) | `Yes`, `true`, `No`, `false`, `In`, `Out` | inborn vs outborn (`In`/`Out`) |
| `mathivtest` | `true`/`Y` and `false`/`N` mixed | single canonical coding |
| `ansteroids` | `"Not Sure"` alongside `Y`/`N`/`U` | `"Not Sure"` → `U` |
| `modedelivery` | numbers `1–7` in some rows, text codes (`SVD`, `ECS`…) in others | one scheme |

Record-level breakdown of the flagged values: **~51.7% mechanical** (same answer written differently — case, `true/false` vs `Y/N`, label vs code, or a valid option from an older form version), **~42.7% unresolved legacy** (options absent from every form version), **~5.6% semantic** (need clinical judgement).

**What is fixed:** the decision-free layer (Module 04 logic + the version-union augmentation in `00_build_dictionary.r`) resolves the ~51.7% mechanical share to canonical codes, information-preservingly, and leaves the rest untouched (never guessed). The canonical dictionaries build **only** from the downloaded web-editor data keys; `VALUEMAP_PATCHES` is reserved for pure data-side legacy variants. **As of 2026-07-17** the clinical-decision layer for the high-volume fields is also encoded (see Section C) — the clinically confirmed legacy→canonical mappings + the 2024 public dictionary now live in `VALUEMAP_PATCHES` and are applied on dictionary rebuild.
**What remains:** a small set of un-mappable/ambiguous legacy values still left as-is (Section C, and the open clinical follow-ups), plus a full systematic public-dictionary sweep of the lower-volume categorical fields.
**Analyst action:** until the master file has been regenerated post-fix, harmonise categorical variables in-script (as the LBW scripts did). Confirm from the pipeline log which run produced your file.

### A2. Mixed script-version coding within a single column

**Variables:** `modedelivery`, Thompson score components, `inorout`, and many categoricals (overlaps A1).
**Status:** FIXED for `modedelivery` and Thompson; OPEN for others.

Because the export mixes app versions, some columns carry two coding systems at once.

**`modedelivery` (ZIM admissions, n≈40k):** numeric codes `1–7` from current scripts mixed with text labels from older scripts (`SVD`, `CSPrLab`, `CSPoLab`, `ID`, `Vent`, `For`, `ECS`, `ElCS`, `IVD`). A `harmonise_modedelivery()` recode was added to Module 04 mapping `SVD→1, Vent→2, For→3, ElCS→4, ECS→5, CSPrLab→5, CSPoLab→5, IVD→7, ID→7`. The 15 May 2026 run recoded 2,667 text labels. Canonical coding: **1=SVD, 2=Vacuum, 3=Forceps, 4=Elective CS, 5=Emergency CS, 6=Breech (vaginal), 7=Induced Vaginal Delivery.** The `col_character()` workaround in analysis scripts can now be removed.
*Compounding bug:* the user dictionary listed this variable as `mode_delivery` (with underscore) while the pipeline column is `modedelivery` (no underscore), so Module 04's dictionary lookup silently missed it — hence the dedicated recode that bypasses the dictionary (see also A5).

**Thompson score fields:** older scripts stored the full descriptive label inline (`"0 = Normal"`, `"1 = Mild"`, `"2 = Moderate"`) while newer scripts store a bare integer. A `strip_thompson_labels()` function (Module 04) strips the ` = <label>` suffix. The 15 May run stripped **148,976** values across 13 Thompson columns.
*Silent-failure sub-bug (worth remembering):* the first implementation matched `^thompson` but the actual columns use the abbreviated prefix `thomp` (`thomptone`, `thompalert`, `thompseiz`, `thomprefl`, `thompmoro`, `thompgrasp`, `thompfeed`, `thompresp`, `thompfont`, `thompscore`). The guard clause returned silently, so the log looked like a clean no-op. Pattern corrected to `^thomp`. Lesson: a guard that returns on zero matches is indistinguishable from a genuine no-op — log a distinct "no columns found" message.

### A3. Legacy variables missing from the dictionary → skipped by validation

**Variables:** `aw` (Admission Weight, if different from birth weight), `bsmmol` (Blood Sugar mmol/L; older equivalent of `bloodsugarmmol`). Potentially others.
**Status:** FIXED (local + DSH).

`cfg$num` is built from dictionary rows with `r_type == "numeric"`. Neither `aw` nor `bsmmol` had a dictionary entry, so both were absent from `cfg$num`. Consequently Module 09 never applied `as.numeric()` and Module 11 never range-checked them — the `"None"` export artefact (see A4) passed through untouched, and no range validation occurred. This was first seen as a `read_csv` type-coercion warning in the Golden Hour analysis.

Fix: both variables added to `dictionary_zim_admissions.xlsx`, **and** — because the Excel dictionaries are regenerated by `00_build_dictionary.r` and manual edits are overwritten — a `LEGACY_VARIABLES` constant was added to the build script so the entries survive rebuilds. Verified: post-fix cleaned ZIM admissions had 0 `"None"` for both; `aw` outside 300–7000 set to NA, `bsmmol` above 40 set to NA.
**Outstanding:** check whether `aw`/`bsmmol` exist in **MWI** admissions raw data; if so add them to `dictionary_mwi_admissions.xlsx` with the same type/ranges.

### A4. PostgreSQL export writes SQL NULL as the string `"None"`

**Variables:** `aw`, `bsmmol` confirmed; potentially any string-typed column.
**Status:** FIXED.

When the database is exported via Python, SQL NULLs can be serialised as Python's `None` → the literal string `"None"` rather than an empty cell. A broad pre-pass was added at the top of `assign_data_types()` (Module 09) that replaces `"None"` with `NA` across all columns before type coercion (intentionally broad to catch future occurrences); the count is logged. Module 16 also lists `"None"` in its `MISSING_STRINGS_16`, so these NAs are correctly coded **-9 (Unknown / SQL NULL)**, not -8 (Invalid/removed).
*(Note: the earlier `modedelivery/Thompson` write-up concluded no fix was needed for `aw`/`bsmmol` on the assumption they were in `cfg$num`; they were not — see A3. The `LEGACY_VARIABLES` + Module 09 pre-pass combination is the definitive fix.)*

### A5. Column-name mismatches between dictionary/data and code

**Status:** PARTIAL.

- **`mode_delivery` vs `modedelivery`** — user dictionary key has an underscore the data column does not; silent Module 04 miss (see A2). Follow-up: correct the user-dictionary key.
- **`BirthWeight.value` vs `Birthweight.value`** — the Malawi raw admissions file uses capital `B`/`W` and singular `value` (`BirthWeight.value`, `BirthWeight.label`), not the lowercase/plural forms sometimes assumed. Case/spelling mismatches like this can cause a pipeline column reference to miss silently — verify exact raw column names before referencing them.

---

## B. Units and numeric-range issues

### B1. Weight unit inconsistency (kg vs g) and the standardisation threshold

**Variables:** all weight columns — `admissionweight`, `birthweight`, `bwtdis`, `aw`, `bwbid`, `dischweight`.
**Status:** FIXED (output now in grams) — but no post-conversion safety net.

Different records store weight in **grams** and others in **kilograms**. Module 11 standardises using a threshold of **20**: a value > 20 is treated as grams (left as-is); a value ≤ 20 is treated as kg (× 1000). This works because neonatal weights sit in a natural "dead zone" — no plausible weight falls between ~6 and ~300 in either unit. The pipeline output is now unified to **grams** (previously kg); logs, reports and the sample-maker birthweight tolerance (now 20 g) were updated accordingly.

Caveats to be aware of:
- **Weights are not range-filtered.** `MANUAL_RANGES` deliberately excludes weight variables (philosophy: retain extreme physiological values for the analyst rather than silently NA them). So there is no floor/ceiling on weight columns — a spurious ambiguous-zone value (e.g. 15 → 15,000 g) is *not* removed. A **non-destructive** "implausible weight after conversion" summary was added to the Module 11 report, but it only warns; it does not change data.
- No dedicated post-conversion birthweight validation (e.g. flagging residual > 8000 g or < 100 g as conversion failures) exists beyond that warning.
**Analyst action:** apply your own weight range filter if your analysis is sensitive to outliers.

### B2. `matageyrs` — maternal age stored in hours for some records

**Variable:** `matageyrs`.
**Status:** FIXED (Module 11 rescue).

The Neotree app sometimes auto-calculates maternal age from date of birth and stores the result in **hours** in `matageyrs` (which should hold **years**) — so values in the hundreds of thousands appear (e.g. 149,325 h ≈ 17 y). It is a **mixed-unit-at-source** problem: manual entry → years, auto-calculation → hours, both land in the same column. The two populations don't overlap (no real maternal age in years exceeds ~60; no hours value is below ~87,600), so Module 11 rescues any value **> 200** by dividing by 8,760 and rounding to 1 d.p., before range validation. Rescued records are flagged in the log.
**Analyst action:** none when using the cleaned master file; only matters if working with raw uncleaned data.

### B3. `age` — age at admission stored in hours, with data-entry outliers

**Variable:** `age` (admission and discharge sides).
**Status:** OPEN — analyst must handle.

`age` is a Neotree `period` ("Days & Hours") field that the pipeline converts to **total hours** (confirmed against `agecategory`: `age=1`→"< 2 hours", `age=24`→"1 day", `age=48`→"2 days"). It must **never** be read as days. A small number of records (44/~27,600 at SMCH, <0.2%) have implausible values up to 291,696 h (~33 y) — data-entry errors. `age` is FLAGGED (see B5) and range validation was deliberately skipped, so these outliers remain in the master file.
**Analyst action:** cap at the neonatal boundary — `age_hours <- if_else(age_hours > 672, NA_real_, age_hours)` (28 days). Do **not** divide large values by 24 — they are errors, not day-encoded values. Use `agecategory` (96% complete at SMCH) as a cross-check.

### B4. `timespent` / `timespent_dis` — form-completion time, not length of stay

**Variables:** `timespent`, `timespent_dis`.
**Status:** OPEN — semantic/interpretation.

These record the clock time (in **hours**) taken to fill in the admission/discharge Neotree form — **not** length of stay. Validated against 0–72 h (retrospective entry can take up to 3 days). SMCH distributions: `timespent` median ≈14 h, max 1,736 h; `timespent_dis` median ≈6 h, max 1,227 h; values > 72 h are out-of-range outliers.
**Analyst action:** use as a LOS proxy only as a fallback (prefer `datetimeadmission`/`datetimedischarge`). If used: `los_days <- timespent/24`, then cap (LBW scripts used 0–180 days).

### B5. Variables FLAGGED "too many out-of-range values" and left unranged

**Status:** OPEN — analyst must handle. **This is a deliberate design choice, not an error code.**

Several variables in `user_ranges.xlsx` carry a manual developer note ("skipped in original validation because too many values fall out of range. Needs Review"). It means the proposed clinical range was tested, too many values fell outside it, and the developer chose to leave the field **unranged** rather than silently drop data. **No values were set to NA for these variables.**

| Variable | Sheet | Range proposed | Flag scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| `age` | Adm + Dis | none set | ZIM + MWI |
| `dischrr` | Dis | none set | ZIM + MWI |
| `bloodsugarmmol` | Adm | max 40 | ZIM + MWI |
| `bloodsugarmg` | Adm | max 600 | MWI only |
| `hr` | Adm | 30–300 | ZIM + MWI |
| `rr` | Adm | max 150 | ZIM + MWI |
| `length` | Adm + Dis | 30–70 | ZIM |
| `wcc1r` | Dis | 5–30 | ZIM |

**Analyst action:** apply your own range filter; the `user_ranges.xlsx` bounds are a reasonable clinical starting point. Whether a defined range was actually applied depends on the run — check the pipeline log (`ZIM_db_pipeline_log_to_*.txt`).

---

## C. Categorical variables needing a clinical mapping decision

**Status (2026-07-17): mostly RESOLVED.** the clinical lead's decision sheet + the 2024 public data dictionary were encoded into `VALUEMAP_PATCHES` (`00_build_dictionary.r`, dated block) and verified on the 25 Jun extract. Each legacy value was mapped **only** to a code that already exists in that field's current canonical set (an alias, never an invented option). Values with no current target, or still clinically ambiguous, remain left-as-is (decision-free) and are tracked as open clinical follow-ups.

**C1 — Coding-scheme / direction decisions — RESOLVED**
- `modedelivery` = `'1'`: **confirmed** numeric `1–7` = SVD/Vacuum/Forceps/ElCS/EmCS/Breech/IVD (old data-dictionary scheme). Text labels already recoded to numeric by `harmonise_modedelivery()`; `'1'` now registered as canonical.
- `inorout` legacy `Yes`/`true` → `In` (inborn); `No`/`false` → `Out` (outborn). **Confirmed** (clinical lead). Encoded for ZIM adm+dis and MWI adm+dis+phc.
- `ortolani` (MWI) legacy `true`/`false` → `Y`/`N`: **already auto-resolved** by the decision-free boolean rule (canonical set is `{Y,N}`); `true`/Yes = abnormality found, `false`/No = none. No patch needed.

**C2 — Unknown admission source — RESOLVED**
- `admittedfrom` = `'ER'`: the 2024 public dictionary defines `ER` = **"External Referral"** as its own option (confirmed — **not** A&E/Casualty). `ER` registered as its own canonical code for ZIM adm+dis and MWI adm+dis. *(Minor: `FCDH` in ZIM admissions, 5 records, is a typo of `FCH` — left untouched, out of scope.)*

**C3 — Legacy Yes/No on fields that are now coded scales — RESOLVED**
All clinically confirmed and encoded: `vomiting` `No`→`Norm`; `murmur` `No`→`NormHSounds`; `punewborn` `Yes`→`YesUr`/`No`→`NoUr`; `matsymptoms` `N`→`Norm`/`O`→`Oth` *(assumption — Tim to confirm)*; `matcomorbidities` `N`→`Norm`/`O`→`Oth` *(assumption — Tim to confirm)*; `receivedantenatalcare` `Y`/`Yes`→`Bkd`/`N`/`No`→`Ubkd`; `cadredis` (MWI) `NO`/`NO - Nursing Officer`→`N` (Nurse).

**C4 — Legacy diagnosis / admission-reason codes — MOSTLY RESOLVED**
Clinician-confirmed mappings encoded, bound to codes already in each field's canonical set; brace multi-selects auto-resolve token-by-token (verified: `{G,LBW,Prem,RDS}`→`{GSchis,LBW,Prem,DIB}`):
- `admreason` (ZIM adm+dis): `O`→`Oth`, `BA`→`HIE`, `LowBirthWeight`/`VLBW`/`ExLBW`→`LBW`, `HBW`→`Mac`, `FD`→`DF`, `TTN`/`TermRD`/`Difficulty in breathing`→`DIB`, `MA`→`PMA`, `sHIE`→`HIE`, `G`/`GSch`→`GSchis`, `OM`→`Omph`, `SEPS`→`NSep`, `VPrem`/`ExPrem`/`Prematurity`→`Prem`, `Mec`→`MecEx`, and `PremRDS`/`PremRD`→`{Prem,DIB}`. (`Low Apgars`→`Apg` auto-resolves via label.)
- `admreasonadd` (ZIM adm): `RDS`→`DIB`, `FD`→`DF`, `G`→`GSchis`.
- `diagdis1` (ZIM dis+baseline): `PR`→`Prem`, `BA`→`HIE`, `PRRDS`→`PremRD`, `FD`→`DF`, `HIVXL`→`HIVLR`, `Ri`→`Risk`, `OCA`→`Cong`, `BI`→`BT`.

**Still pending (no current target / clinically ambiguous — open follow-ups):** `admreason` `Safe`/`Safekeeping` (candidate `Mothnotavail`?), `Hyperth`, `HIVLR`; `admreasonadd` `Mec`, `Cong`; `diagdis1` `HBW` (no macrosomia option), `JAUN` (physiological vs pathological). Related fields not in the clinical decision sheet (`othprobs`, `contcausedeath`, `causedeath`, ZIM `cadredis`/`ortolani`, MWI `admreason`/`diagdis1`) are queued for the next round.

**C5 — Free-text / "Other" policy — STILL OPEN**
~11,299 distinct one-off values (free-text, typos, "Other, specify" text). the clinical lead left the decision box blank; proposal to route all to NA/`Oth` is **not yet confirmed or implemented**. Values remain untouched (decision-free).

## D. Weight / birthweight variable semantics (easy to misread)

**Status:** clarified — documented here to prevent misuse.

- **`birthweight`, `bwt`, `bwtdis` all denote the same concept — birth weight** — and can be coalesced safely. Note `bwtdis` is **birth weight**, *not* discharge weight, despite the `dis` in its name.
- **`admissionweight` (`aw`) and `dischweight` are distinct concepts** from birth weight and from each other — do not coalesce them with birth weight.
- **`birthweight.value` vs `.valuedischarge`:** `.valuedischarge` does **not** exist in any current data — no dictionary, data key, or script produces it. The pipeline strips `.valuedischarge` throughout (modules 04/05/06/09/11/13/15 and `00b`) purely as defensive future-proofing for a combined admission+discharge form that never materialised. For birthweight, only `.value` is ever stripped, always matching the single birthweight dictionary row. The real discharge-only fields use a `dc` suffix (`hivtestresultdc`, `apgar1dc`, `gestationdc`…), a separate mechanism.
- **No value is lost across admission/discharge.** Admissions and discharges are cleaned as separate runs; they are joined only at analysis time (`joiner.R`) on `uid + facility`, with clashing discharge columns suffixed `_dis` (so `birthweight` = admission, `birthweight_dis` = discharge). Both are preserved; unmatched records are kept as separate tables.
- **Raw double-entry observation:** in the MWI raw admissions file, 4,380 rows had a numeric weight in `BirthWeight.label` (e.g. "3000") as well as in `BirthWeight.value` — the label field should hold descriptive text, not a number. Worth checking those two agree.

---

## E. Derived "combined" variables created by the pipeline

**Status:** implemented — these are the pipeline's own mitigation for the multi-name problem. Use them in analysis.

To resolve the problem that the same clinical concept was recorded under **different variable names across Neotree form/script versions**, the cleaning pipeline creates new canonical variables that coalesce all the historical names into one, **leaving every original source column in place**. They are computed in **Module 15** (final merge), *after* all validation modules, so they carry no raw `.value`/`.label` column and are skipped by Modules 04/09/11. All weight variants are in **grams** (post kg→g standardisation, see B1) with a documented plausibility range of 300–7000 g.

Two families exist:

**Weight** — function `derive_weight_columns()` in `15_final_merge_output.r`:

| Derived variable | Coalesce logic (priority order) | Emitted in |
|---|---|---|
| `birthweight_g` | `coalesce(birthweight, bwt, bwtdis)` — three historical names for the same concept | **every** dataset, both sites (NA-filled where no source is present, so all cleaned files share a common schema) |
| `admission_weight_g` | `admissionweight` only | admission-type forms (admissions, phc_admissions, baseline [ZIM], joined) |
| `discharge_weight_g` | `dischweight` only (date in `datedischweight`) | discharge-type forms |

Only `birthweight_g` is a genuine multi-name coalesce. `admission_weight_g` and `discharge_weight_g` are single-source canonical renames — deliberately **never folded into birth weight**, because they are distinct clinical concepts (recall from D that `bwtdis` *is* birth weight despite its name, whereas `admissionweight`/`dischweight` are not).

**Maternal age** — function `derive_maternal_age_columns()` in `15_final_merge_output.r`:

| Derived variable | Coalesce logic | Emitted in |
|---|---|---|
| `mat_age_years_combined` | `coalesce(matageyrs, mat_age_date_years)` — manually-entered years, falling back to the `matagedate` value (auto-calculated from DOB in hours, converted to years and range-cleaned in Module 11; see B2) | deliveries / maternal-outcomes files only (where a source column exists) |
| `mat_age_source` | provenance label for the above: `"matageyrs"` / `"matagedate_derived"` / `"none"` | alongside `mat_age_years_combined` |

**Properties to know:**

- **Originals are retained** — the derived column is additive; source columns are untouched, so provenance and raw values remain auditable.
- **Lossless coalesce with a disagreement guard.** Where two sources overlap and disagree (weight beyond a gram tolerance; maternal age by > 1 year), the rows are **counted and logged**, never silently overwritten. The priority/first source wins (`matageyrs` beats the DOB-derived value, per the form instruction to leave `matageyrs` blank only when the auto value is used).
- **`birthweight_g` is the column to use for low-birth-weight analysis** — it is the canonical, always-present, gram-scale birth weight.
- **Dictionary registration:** `birthweight_g`, `admission_weight_g`, `discharge_weight_g` are registered in the `DERIVED_VARIABLES` block of `00_build_dictionary.r` (documentation-only; they flow into the researcher-facing user dictionary via `00d`). **Gap noted:** `mat_age_years_combined` and `mat_age_source` are created in Module 15 but do **not** appear in that `DERIVED_VARIABLES` list — so they exist in the cleaned output yet may be undocumented in the dictionary. Worth adding for completeness.

---

## F. Missingness — why a cell is NA

**Status:** documented; NA reason codes implemented (Module 16).

Not all NAs mean the same thing. Missing cells arise from several distinct mechanisms; the confirmed legitimate causes are:

1. Originally empty in the raw file (never filled in).
2. Not applicable by design (question wasn't shown — e.g. a test result where the test wasn't performed).
3. Literal missing-value strings normalised to NA by Module 13 (`nan`, `none`, `null`, `n/a`, `""`, and — for the export artefact — `None`).
4. Invalid / unrecognised dictionary value replaced with NA (Module 04).
5. Label contamination — the question text itself ended up in a coded cell, set to NA (Module 04).
6. PII redaction (Module 00a) — values matching phone/email/NHS/hospital-number patterns are wiped (e.g. the KCH `hivtestresult` PII-stripping fix).
7. Out-of-range numeric value replaced with NA (Module 11) — for variables that *have* a range (contrast with B5).
8. Type-coercion failures (Modules 09/11/12/14) — unparseable values silently become NA via `as.numeric()`/`parse_date_time()`/boolean mapping.
9. Pattern-based contamination (Module 13) — title-case header-like strings and ISO/word timestamps NA'd out.
10. Disallowed-values blacklist (`cfg$values_to_delete`, Module 13) — optional.

Two mechanisms initially listed were **discounted** on review: (a) three-key join mismatches in Module 15 — only a risk if Module 10 deduplication misses duplicates and per-subframe dedup picks different `uniquekey`s; under normal flow the three-key join (`facility, uid, uniquekey`) is equivalent to a two-key join and produces no NAs (see G2); (b) the POSIXct→character `"NA"` artefact in Module 15 — a safeguard that preserves existing NAs, not a new source.

**NA reason codes** (negative sentinels placed in each missing cell; priority order highest→lowest):

| Code | Label | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| **-6** | Redacted | raw value matched a PII pattern (overrides everything) |
| **-7** | Not Applicable | skip logic shows the field wasn't shown; exclude from completeness analyses |
| **-8** | Invalid/Removed | non-empty, non-PII value failed validation; rare — flags a data-quality issue |
| **-9** | Unknown | field was applicable but no value recorded (includes SQL NULL / `None`) |

**Analyst action:** before excluding a high-missingness variable, check whether the missingness is mostly **-7** (structural/expected) rather than genuine gaps. Raw removed values are recoverable from `{stem}_na_reasons_long.csv.gz`.

---

## G. Duplicates and joins

### G1. Duplicate-UID rows in raw data (correctly removed, but worth understanding)

**Status:** working as intended; source-side data question.

In the MWI raw admissions file, cleaning dropped 746 duplicate-UID rows; 431 of them carried a birthweight, fully accounting for a 430-value gap between raw and cleaned `birthweight` counts. So the cleaning logic loses no birthweight values — every dropped value belonged to a duplicate row correctly removed (Module 10 keeps the most-complete copy per `uid + facility`). The open question is upstream: **why** these duplicate submissions exist (re-submissions, entry errors, sync issues).

### G2. Three-key vs two-key join in the final merge (Module 15)

**Status:** latent risk only.

`KEY_COLS <- c("facility","uid","uniquekey")` is used system-wide. Module 10 already deduplicates to one row per `uid + facility`, so by Module 15 each pair has exactly one `uniquekey` and the three-key join is equivalent to a two-key join. It would only produce spurious NAs if Module 10 missed duplicates *and* different type-subframes independently kept rows with different `uniquekey`s. Narrow edge case, not a routine NA source — but the stricter three-key join fails "visibly" (NAs) rather than silently duplicating rows.

---

## H. Mandatory fields that nonetheless allow "Unknown / Not Sure"

**Status:** documented (extracted 16 July 2026).

**100 mandatory Neotree fields** have a valid "Unknown / Not Sure" answer option — identified in a 2026-07-16 audit of the data keys. This matters because a field being mandatory does **not** guarantee a substantive value: an "Unknown"/"Not Sure" response is a legitimate, non-missing answer that should not be conflated with a real clinical value nor with a true NA. Handle these explicitly in completeness and analysis logic.

**Planned fix — new NA-reason code `-5` "documented unknown"** (agreed 2026-07-16): keep an explicitly-selected "Unknown" answer on a mandatory field distinct from `-8` (invalid) and `-9` (genuinely blank). **BLOCKED** on the confirmed field list before implementation in `16_na_reason_coding.r` — see `fixes/pending/NA-codes/NA_reason_code_minus5_fix.md`. Prep done (2026-07-17): the 100 fields were enriched with question text, skip-logic dependency (public dict + JSON), and dictionary-vs-JSON disagreement flags → `fixes/pending/NA-codes/Mandatory_fields_ENRICHED_20260717.xlsx` (55/100 fields are absent from the 2024 public dictionary; none carry a documented rename).

---

## Appendix — dataset-specific notes

- **ZIM infections dataset:** a server-side *derived* file with **no Neotree app script** behind it. `Transformed = TRUE` on all rows (expected). Low `00c` script-enrichment (2/21 fields enriched) is **expected**, not an error, because there is no source script to enrich from.
- **MWI maternal datasets:** the DHIS2-based maternal script is live (2024–present); the original maternal script was retired January 2022. `maternity_completeness` is derived from **paper records**. `neotreeoutcome` UUID-override warnings for `maternal_outcomes` / `maternity_completeness` are **benign** — the field is simply absent from those forms.

---
